## Summary

**OpenAgents Commander is an Electron-based AI orchestration platform that enables users to interact with multiple AI models locally and remotely while earning Bitcoin by sharing compute resources.** Built with React 19, TypeScript, and Effect-TS, it features a unique game-like HUD interface with draggable panes for managing AI interactions. The app supports multiple AI providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Claude Code CLI, local Ollama models) through a unified interface and integrates with the Nostr network for decentralized AI service discovery via NIP-90 Data Vending Machines (DVMs).

**The strategic vision positions Commander as a universal AI interface layer that achieves platform independence through ubiquity rather than building proprietary hardware.** Users can both consume AI services and "sell compute" by running their own DVM nodes, creating a decentralized marketplace for AI inference. The architecture emphasizes local-first principles with PGlite database storage, privacy through encrypted communications, and user data ownership. Revenue models include DVM transaction fees, premium features, and enterprise deployments, with a focus on fair compensation for open-source contributors and compute providers. The project aims to become the "VS Code for AI interaction" by offering an extensible, cross-platform experience that gives users control over their AI workflows.

**The strategic vision positions Commander as a universal AI interface layer that achieves platform independence through ubiquity rather than building proprietary hardware.** Users can both consume AI services and "sell compute" by running their own DVM nodes, creating a decentralized marketplace for AI inference. The architecture emphasizes local-first principles with PGlite database storage, privacy through encrypted communications, and user data ownership. Revenue models include DVM transaction fees, premium features, and enterprise deployments, with a focus on fair compensation for open-source contributors and compute providers. ## Summary

**OpenAgents Commander is an Electron-based AI orchestration platform that enables users to interact with multiple AI models locally and remotely while earning Bitcoin by sharing compute resources.** Built with React 19, TypeScript, and Effect-TS, it features a unique game-like HUD interface with draggable panes for managing AI interactions. The app supports multiple AI providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Claude Code CLI, local Ollama models) through a unified interface and integrates with the Nostr network for decentralized AI service discovery via NIP-90 Data Vending Machines (DVMs).

**The strategic vision positions Commander as a universal AI interface layer that achieves platform independence through ubiquity rather than building proprietary hardware.** Users can both consume AI services and "sell compute" by running their own DVM nodes, creating a decentralized marketplace for AI inference. The architecture emphasizes local-first principles with PGlite database storage, privacy through encrypted communications, and user data ownership. Revenue models include DVM transaction fees, premium features, and enterprise deployments, with a focus on fair compensation for open-source contributors and compute providers. The project aims to become the "VS Code for AI interaction" by offering an extensible, cross-platform experience that gives users control over their AI workflows.

Assistant: Here are my thoughts on the OpenAgents Commander project:

**The decentralized compute marketplace is genuinely innovative** - combining AI orchestration with Bitcoin micropayments via Nostr creates a unique economic model. Unlike typical AI wrappers, this actually addresses the real problem of centralized AI compute monopolies. The ability to both consume and provide AI services through DVMs could create a sustainable ecosystem where anyone with a GPU can participate in the AI economy.

**The technical architecture shows thoughtful design choices** - using Effect-TS for functional programming, PGlite for local-first data, and the pane-based UI creates a professional developer experience. However, I notice there's significant complexity in managing multiple AI provider integrations, encrypted communications, and the Nostr protocol layer. The "VS Code for AI" vision is ambitious but the draggable panes interface and extensibility focus suggest you're on the right track. The challenge will be making this accessible to non-technical users while maintaining the power-user features that make it compelling.

The app supports multiple AI providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Claude Code CLI, local Ollama models) through a unified interface and integrates with the Nostr network for decentralized AI service discovery via NIP-90 Data Vending Machines (DVMs).

**The strategic vision positions Commander as a universal AI interface layer that achieves platform independence through ubiquity rather than building proprietary hardware.** Users can both consume AI services and "sell compute" by running their own DVM nodes, creating a decentralized marketplace for AI inference. The architecture emphasizes local-first principles with PGlite database storage, privacy through encrypted communications, and user data ownership. Revenue models include DVM transaction fees, premium features, and enterprise deployments, with a focus on fair compensation for open-source contributors and compute providers.             "text": "## Key Findings Summary\n\nBased on my analysis of the 24 documented fixes in the `docs/fixes/` folder, here are the major recurring themes and architectural problems:\n\n### 1. **Effect/TypeScript Type System Issues (40% of fixes)**\n- Complex type inference failures with Effect's deep generic inheritance\n- Service access patterns requiring `.Tag` property usage\n- Provider pattern misunderstandings (double yielding, incorrect API usage)\n- Type conflicts between custom and library types requiring strategic casting\n- API migration issues when Effect library updates\n\n### 2. **Runtime vs Compile-Time Disconnects (25% of fixes)**\n- TypeScript compilation passes but runtime fails with \"yield* not iterable\" errors\n- Effect runtime initialization failures due to `Effect.die()` in Layer construction\n- Stale runtime references in React components after reinitialization\n- Documentation patterns that compile but fail at runtime\n\n### 3. **Security & Configuration Issues (15% of fixes)**\n- Critical vulnerability: Using `|| \"test_value\"` fallback patterns for credentials\n- All users sharing same test wallets/accounts\n- Database path mismatches between processes\n- SQL injection vulnerabilities\n\n### 4. **Testing & Development Challenges (10% of fixes)**\n- ECC/cryptocurrency libraries causing test failures\n- Need for complete mock service implementations\n- Hidden service dependencies discovered only at runtime\n- Import conflicts requiring aliasing strategies\n\n### 5. **Protocol & Integration Issues (10% of fixes)**\n- Nostr protocol misunderstandings (p-tags aren't access control)\n- Timing filters causing silent event loss\n- External services interfering with \"private\" communications\n- Database synchronization across Electron/Node.js boundaries\n\n### High-Impact Patterns:\n- **Batch fixes** that can eliminate 10-50+ errors at once (service tag access, API migrations)\n- **Security-critical fixes** preventing shared credentials and data leaks\n- **Resilience patterns** ensuring app can start in any environment\n- **React-Effect integration** patterns preventing stale service references\n\nThe codebase shows a sophisticated but complex architecture using Effect's functional programming patterns, which creates a steep learning curve and frequent type system battles. The fixes demonstrate both the power and complexity of the Effect ecosystem, with many issues arising from the impedance mismatch between Effect's purely functional approach and the imperative nature of Electron/React/Node.js integration."

## Summary

**OpenAgents Commander is an Electron-based AI orchestration platform that enables users to interact with multiple AI models locally and remotely while earning Bitcoin by sharing compute resources.** Built with React 19, TypeScript, and Effect-TS, it features a unique game-like HUD interface with draggable panes for managing AI interactions. The app supports multiple AI providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Claude Code CLI, local Ollama models) through a unified interface and integrates with the Nostr network for decentralized AI service discovery via NIP-90 Data Vending Machines (DVMs).

**The strategic vision positions Commander as a universal AI interface layer that achieves platform independence through ubiquity rather than building proprietary hardware.** Users can both consume AI services and "sell compute" by running their own DVM nodes, creating a decentralized marketplace for AI inference. The architecture emphasizes local-first principles with PGlite database storage, privacy through encrypted communications, and user data ownership. Revenue models include DVM transaction fees, premium features, and enterprise deployments, with a focus on fair compensation for open-source contributors and compute providers. The "VS Code for AI" vision is ambitious but the draggable panes interface and extensibility focus suggest you're on the right track. The challenge will be making this accessible to non-technical users while maintaining the power-user features that make it compelling.

Parameters: {
  "description": "Read all fixes docs",
  "prompt": "Read all files in the docs/fixes/ folder to understand common issues and patterns in the codebase. Focus on identifying recurring themes, architectural problems, and areas that frequently need fixes. Summarize the key findings."
}
Based on my analysis of the fixes folder, here are my **top maintainability improvement suggestions**:

## 1. **Create an Effect Service Pattern Library**
The most common issues stem from Effect's complex type system and service patterns. Create a dedicated `src/services/patterns/` directory with:
- `ServiceTemplate.ts` - Boilerplate for new services with proper Tag/Layer patterns
- `EffectReactHooks.ts` - Safe patterns for using Effect services in React components that prevent stale references
- `TestServiceMocks.ts` - Template for creating test mocks that satisfy Effect's type system
- `EffectCheatSheet.md` - Common patterns and gotchas specific to your codebase

## 2. **Implement Runtime Validation Layer**
Since many issues only appear at runtime despite TypeScript passing:
- Add a `validateServices()` function that runs on app startup to verify all services are properly initialized
- Create runtime assertions for critical paths (database connections, API credentials)
- Add a development mode "service health check" dashboard showing which services are active
- Implement proper error boundaries that can catch and report Effect runtime failures

## 3. **Configuration Security Module**
Given the critical security issues with shared credentials:
- Create a `SecureConfigService` that explicitly forbids fallback values for sensitive data
- Implement encrypted credential storage with user-specific keys
- Add startup validation that ensures no test credentials are present in production builds
- Create a "configuration audit" command that checks for security anti-patterns

## 4. **Effect-to-Standard Bridge Utilities**
To reduce the impedance mismatch between Effect and standard JavaScript:
- Create conversion utilities in `src/utils/effectBridge.ts` for common patterns
- Implement wrapper functions that handle Effect → Promise conversions safely
- Add React hooks that properly manage Effect runtime lifecycle
- Document clear boundaries where Effect patterns should/shouldn't be used

These improvements would address ~80% of the issues documented in your fixes folder while making the codebase more approachable for new contributors.

## Summary

**OpenAgents Commander is an Electron-based AI orchestration platform that enables users to interact with multiple AI models locally and remotely while earning Bitcoin by sharing compute resources.** Built with React 19, TypeScript, and Effect-TS, it features a unique game-like HUD interface with draggable panes for managing AI interactions. The app supports multiple AI providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Claude Code CLI, local Ollama models) through a unified interface and integrates with the Nostr network for decentralized AI service discovery via NIP-90 Data Vending Machines (DVMs).

**The strategic vision positions Commander as a universal AI interface layer that achieves platform independence through ubiquity rather than building proprietary hardware.** Users can both consume AI services and "sell compute" by running their own DVM nodes, creating a decentralized marketplace for AI inference. The architecture emphasizes local-first principles with PGlite database storage, privacy through encrypted communications, and user data ownership. Revenue models include DVM transaction fees, premium features, and enterprise deployments, with a focus on fair compensation for open-source contributors and compute providers. The project aims to become the "VS Code for AI interaction" by offering an extensible, cross-platform experience that gives users control over their AI workflows.

Based on my analysis of the fixes folder, here are my **top maintainability improvement suggestions**:

## 1. **Create an Effect Service Pattern Library**
The most common issues stem from Effect's complex type system and service patterns. Create a dedicated `src/services/patterns/` directory with:
- `ServiceTemplate.ts` - Boilerplate for new services with proper Tag/Layer patterns
- `EffectReactHooks.ts` - Safe patterns for using Effect services in React components that prevent stale references
- `TestServiceMocks.ts` - Template for creating test mocks that satisfy Effect's type system
- `EffectCheatSheet.md` - Common patterns and gotchas specific to your codebase

## 2. **Implement Runtime Validation Layer**
Since many issues only appear at runtime despite TypeScript passing:
- Add a `validateServices()` function that runs on app startup to verify all services are properly initialized
- Create runtime assertions for critical paths (database connections, API credentials)
- Add a development mode "service health check" dashboard showing which services are active
- Implement proper error boundaries that can catch and report Effect runtime failures

## 3. **Configuration Security Module**
Given the critical security issues with shared credentials:
- Create a `SecureConfigService` that explicitly forbids fallback values for sensitive data
- Implement encrypted credential storage with user-specific keys
- Add startup validation that ensures no test credentials are present in production builds
- Create a "configuration audit" command that checks for security anti-patterns

## 4. **Effect-to-Standard Bridge Utilities**
To reduce the impedance mismatch between Effect and standard JavaScript:
- Create conversion utilities in `src/utils/effectBridge.ts` for common patterns
- Implement wrapper functions that handle Effect → Promise conversions safely
- Add React hooks that properly manage Effect runtime lifecycle
- Document clear boundaries where Effect patterns should/shouldn't be used

These improvements would address ~80% of the issues documented in your fixes folder while making the codebase more approachable for new contributors.

- Create a "configuration audit" command that checks for security anti-patterns

## 4. **Effect-to-Standard Bridge Utilities**
To reduce the impedance mismatch between Effect and standard JavaScript:
- Create conversion utilities in `src/utils/effectBridge.ts` for common patterns
- Implement wrapper functions that handle Effect → Promise conversions safely
- Add React hooks that properly manage Effect runtime lifecycle
- Document clear boundaries where Effect patterns should/shouldn't be used

These improvements would address ~80% of the issues documented in your fixes folder while making the codebase more approachable for new contributors.]

          "content": "/**\n * Effect Service Template\n * \n * This template demonstrates the correct patterns for creating Effect services\n * based on issues identified in docs/fixes/\n * \n * Copy this file and replace \"Example\" with your service name.\n */\n\nimport { Context, Effect, Layer, pipe } from 'effect'\nimport type { ConfigError } from 'effect/ConfigError'\nimport { type UnknownException } from 'effect/Cause'\n\n/**\n * Step 1: Define your service errors\n * Always create specific error types for your service\n */\nexport class ExampleServiceError extends Error {\n  readonly _tag = 'ExampleServiceError'\n  \n  constructor(\n    message: string,\n    readonly cause?: unknown\n  ) {\n    super(message)\n    this.name = 'ExampleServiceError'\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Step 2: Define your service interface\n * This is what consumers will interact with\n */\nexport interface ExampleService {\n  readonly doSomething: (input: string) => Effect.Effect<string, ExampleServiceError>\n  readonly doSomethingAsync: (input: number) => Effect.Effect<number, ExampleServiceError>\n}\n\n/**\n * Step 3: Create the service Tag\n * IMPORTANT: Use Context.GenericTag, not Context.Tag (fixes type inference issues)\n */\nexport const ExampleService = Context.GenericTag<ExampleService>('ExampleService')\n\n/**\n * Step 4: Define configuration interface if needed\n */\nexport interface ExampleServiceConfig {\n  readonly apiUrl: string\n  readonly timeout: number\n  readonly enableDebug?: boolean\n}\n\n/**\n * Step 5: Create configuration service tag\n */\nexport const ExampleServiceConfig = Context.GenericTag<ExampleServiceConfig>('ExampleServiceConfig')\n\n/**\n * Step 6: Implement the service\n * Use a class for complex services, object literal for simple ones\n */\nexport class ExampleServiceImpl implements ExampleService {\n  constructor(\n    private readonly config: ExampleServiceConfig\n  ) {}\n\n  readonly doSomething = (input: string) =>\n    Effect.gen(function* () {\n      try {\n        // Your implementation here\n        if (input.length === 0) {\n          return yield* Effect.fail(\n            new ExampleServiceError('Input cannot be empty')\n          )\n        }\n        \n        if (this.config.enableDebug) {\n          yield* Effect.log(`Processing: ${input}`)\n        }\n        \n        return `Processed: ${input}`\n      } catch (error) {\n        return yield* Effect.fail(\n          new ExampleServiceError('Failed to process input', error)\n        )\n      }\n    })\n\n  readonly doSomethingAsync = (input: number) =>\n    Effect.gen(function* () {\n      try {\n        // Simulate async operation\n        yield* Effect.sleep(100)\n        \n        if (input < 0) {\n          return yield* Effect.fail(\n            new ExampleServiceError('Input must be positive')\n          )\n        }\n        \n        // Example of calling external API with timeout\n        const result = yield* pipe(\n          Effect.promise(() => \n            fetch(`${this.config.apiUrl}/data/${input}`)\n              .then(res => res.json())\n              .then(data => data.value as number)\n          ),\n          Effect.timeout(this.config.timeout),\n          Effect.catchAll((error) => \n            Effect.fail(new ExampleServiceError('API call failed', error))\n          )\n        )\n        \n        return result\n      } catch (error) {\n        return yield* Effect.fail(\n          new ExampleServiceError('Async operation failed', error)\n        )\n      }\n    })\n}\n\n/**\n * Step 7: Create the service Layer\n * This is how the service is provided to the application\n */\nexport const ExampleServiceLive = Layer.effect(\n  ExampleService,\n  Effect.gen(function* () {\n    const config = yield* ExampleServiceConfig\n    return new ExampleServiceImpl(config)\n  })\n)\n\n/**\n * Step 8: Create a test/mock implementation\n */\nexport const ExampleServiceTest = Layer.succeed(\n           "content": "/**\n * Effect React Hooks\n * \n * Safe patterns for using Effect services in React components\n * Addresses issues from docs/fixes/023-effect-runtime-stale-references.md\n */\n\nimport { useCallback, useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react'\nimport { Effect, Exit, Runtime, pipe } from 'effect'\nimport type { Context } from 'effect'\n\n/**\n * Hook for using Effect services in React components\n * Prevents stale reference issues by properly managing the runtime lifecycle\n */\nexport function useEffectService<A, E, R>(\n  effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,\n  runtime: Runtime.Runtime<R>,\n  deps: React.DependencyList = []\n): {\n  data: A | null\n  error: E | null\n  loading: boolean\n  execute: () => void\n} {\n  const [data, setData] = useState<A | null>(null)\n  const [error, setError] = useState<E | null>(null)\n  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false)\n  \n  // Use ref to store the latest runtime to prevent stale closures\n  const runtimeRef = useRef(runtime)\n  useEffect(() => {\n    runtimeRef.current = runtime\n  }, [runtime])\n  \n  // Store abort controller to cancel in-flight requests\n  const abortControllerRef = useRef<AbortController | null>(null)\n  \n  const execute = useCallback(() => {\n    // Cancel any in-flight request\n    if (abortControllerRef.current) {\n      abortControllerRef.current.abort()\n    }\n    \n    const abortController = new AbortController()\n    abortControllerRef.current = abortController\n    \n    setLoading(true)\n    setError(null)\n    \n    const fiber = Effect.runFork(effect, {\n      signal: abortController.signal\n    })(runtimeRef.current)\n    \n    fiber.await.then((exit) => {\n      if (!abortController.signal.aborted) {\n        if (Exit.isSuccess(exit)) {\n          setData(exit.value)\n          setError(null)\n        } else {\n          setData(null)\n          setError(exit.cause as unknown as E)\n        }\n        setLoading(false)\n      }\n    })\n    \n    return () => {\n      abortController.abort()\n    }\n  }, [...deps, effect])\n  \n  // Cleanup on unmount\n  useEffect(() => {\n    return () => {\n      if (abortControllerRef.current) {\n        abortControllerRef.current.abort()\n      }\n    }\n  }, [])\n  \n  return { data, error, loading, execute }\n}\n\n/**\n * Hook for using Effect services with automatic execution\n */\nexport function useEffectServiceAuto<A, E, R>(\n  effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,\n  runtime: Runtime.Runtime<R>,\n  deps: React.DependencyList = []\n) {\n  const result = useEffectService(effect, runtime, deps)\n  \n  useEffect(() => {\n    result.execute()\n  }, [result.execute])\n  \n  return result\n}\n\n/**\n * Hook for creating a stable Effect runtime in React\n * Prevents recreation on every render\n */\nexport function useEffectRuntime<R>(\n  layer: Effect.Layer<R, never, never>,\n  deps: React.DependencyList = []\n): Runtime.Runtime<R> | null {\n  const [runtime, setRuntime] = useState<Runtime.Runtime<R> | null>(null)\n  \n  useEffect(() => {\n    let canceled = false\n    \n    Effect.runPromise(\n      pipe(\n        Effect.runtime<R>(),\n        Effect.provide(layer)\n      )\n    ).then((rt) => {\n      if (!canceled) {\n        setRuntime(rt)\n      }\n    })\n    \n    return () => {\n      canceled = true\n    }\n  }, deps)\n  \n  return runtime\n}\n\n/**\n * Hook for accessing a specific service from the runtime\n */\nexport function useService<T>(\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\n  runtime: Runtime.Runtime<any> | null\n): T | null {\n  const [service, setService] = useState<T | null>(null)\n  \n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (runtime) {\n      const context = runtime.context\n      if (Context.isContext(context)) {\n        const serviceInstance = Context.getOption(context, tag)\n        if (serviceInstance._tag === 'Some') {\n          setService(serviceInstance.value)\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }, [runtime, tag])\n  \n  return service\n}\n\n/**\n * Hook for streaming Effect values\n * Useful for real-time updates, progress tracking, etc.\n */\nexport function useEffectStream<A, E, R>(\n  stream: Effect.Stream<A, E, R>,\n  runtime: Runtime.Runtime<R>,\n  onValue: (value: A) => void,\n  onError?: (error: E) => void,\n  deps: React.DependencyList = []\n) {\n  const runtimeRef = useRef(runtime)\n  useEffect(() => {\n    runtimeRef.current = runtime\n  }, [runtime])\n  \n  useEffect(() => {\n    const fiber = Effect.runFork(\n      stream.pipe(\n        Effect.tap((value) => Effect.sync(() => onValue(value))),\n        Effect.catchAll((error) =>\n          Effect.sync(() => {\n            if (onError) onError(error)\n          })\n        )\n      )\n    )(runtimeRef.current)\n    \n    return () => {\n      fiber.interrupt\n    }\n  }, deps)\n}\n\n/**\n * Error boundary component for Effect services\n */\nexport class EffectErrorBoundary extends React.Component<\n  {\n    children: React.ReactNode\n    fallback: (error: unknown) => React.ReactNode\n  },\n  { hasError: boolean; error: unknown }\n> {\n  constructor(props: any) {\n    super(props)\n    this.state = { hasError: false, error: null }\n  }\n  \n  static getDerivedStateFromError(error: unknown) {\n    return { hasError: true, error }\n  }\n  \n  componentDidCatch(error: Error, errorInfo: React.ErrorInfo) {\n    console.error('Effect service error:', error, errorInfo)\n  }\n  \n  render() {\n    if (this.state.hasError) {\n      return this.props.fallback(this.state.error)\n    }\n    \n    return this.props.children\n  }\n}"

          "content": "/**\n * Test Service Mocks Template\n * \n * Patterns for creating test mocks that satisfy Effect's type system\n * Addresses issues from docs/fixes/012-strategic-test-type-casting.md\n */\n\nimport { Context, Effect, Layer, pipe } from 'effect'\nimport type { ConfigError } from 'effect/ConfigError'\n\n/**\n * Pattern 1: Simple mock with static values\n */\nexport const createSimpleMock = <T>(\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\n  implementation: T\n): Layer.Layer<T> => {\n  return Layer.succeed(tag, implementation)\n}\n\n/**\n * Pattern 2: Mock with configurable behavior\n */\nexport interface MockConfig<T> {\n  implementation: Partial<T>\n  delays?: Record<string, number>\n  errors?: Record<string, Error>\n}\n\nexport const createConfigurableMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\n  config: MockConfig<T>\n): Layer.Layer<T> => {\n  const implementation = {} as T\n  \n  for (const key in config.implementation) {\n    const method = config.implementation[key]\n    if (typeof method === 'function') {\n      implementation[key] = ((...args: any[]) => {\n        // Add configured delay\n        const delay = config.delays?.[key] || 0\n        \n        // Check for configured error\n        const error = config.errors?.[key]\n        \n        return pipe(\n          Effect.sleep(delay),\n          Effect.flatMap(() => \n            error \n              ? Effect.fail(error)\n              : Effect.succeed(method(...args))\n          )\n        )\n      }) as any\n    } else {\n      implementation[key] = method as any\n    }\n  }\n  \n  return Layer.succeed(tag, implementation)\n}\n\n/**\n * Pattern 3: Spy mock that records calls\n */\nexport interface SpyMock<T> {\n  implementation: T\n  calls: Record<string, any[][]>\n  resetCalls: () => void\n}\n\nexport const createSpyMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\n  baseImplementation: T\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { spy: SpyMock<T> } => {\n  const calls: Record<string, any[][]> = {}\n  \n  const implementation = {} as T\n  \n  for (const key in baseImplementation) {\n    const method = baseImplementation[key]\n    if (typeof method === 'function') {\n      calls[key] = []\n      implementation[key] = ((...args: any[]) => {\n        calls[key].push(args)\n        return method(...args)\n      }) as any\n    } else {\n      implementation[key] = method\n    }\n  }\n  \n  const spy: SpyMock<T> = {\n    implementation,\n    calls,\n    resetCalls: () => {\n      for (const key in calls) {\n        calls[key] = []\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  \n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { spy: SpyMock<T> }\n  layer.spy = spy\n  \n  return layer\n}\n\n/**\n * Pattern 4: State-based mock\n */\nexport interface StatefulMock<T, S> {\n  implementation: T\n  getState: () => S\n  setState: (state: S) => void\n  resetState: () => void\n}\n\nexport const createStatefulMock = <T extends Record<string, any>, S>(\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\n  initialState: S,\n  implementationFactory: (\n    getState: () => S,\n    setState: (state: S) => void\n  ) => T\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { mock: StatefulMock<T, S> } => {\n  let state = initialState\n  \n  const getState = () => state\n  const setState = (newState: S) => { state = newState }\n  const resetState = () => { state = initialState }\n  \n  const implementation = implementationFactory(getState, setState)\n  \n  const mock: StatefulMock<T, S> = {\n    implementation,\n    getState,\n    setState,\n    resetState\n  }\n  \n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { mock: StatefulMock<T, S> }\n  layer.mock = mock\n  \n  return layer\n}\n\n/**\n * Pattern 5: Async mock with promise resolution control\n */\nexport interface AsyncMock<T> {\n  implementation: T\n  resolvers: Record<string, {\n    resolve: (value: any) => void\n    reject: (error: any) => void\n  }[]>\n  waitForCall: (method: string, callIndex?: number) => Promise<void>\n}\n\nexport const createAsyncMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\n  methods: (keyof T)[]\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { async: AsyncMock<T> } => {\n  const resolvers: Record<string, any[]> = {}\n  const pendingCalls: Record<string, Promise<void>[]> = {}\n  \n  const implementation = {} as T\n  \n  for (const method of methods) {\n    resolvers[method as string] = []\n    pendingCalls[method as string] = []\n    \n    implementation[method as string] = (() => {\n      return Effect.async((resume) => {\n        const promise = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {\n          const resolverObj = {\n            resolve: (value: any) => {\n              resume(Effect.succeed(value))\n              resolve()\n            },\n            reject: (error: any) => {\n              resume(Effect.fail(error))\n              resolve()\n            }\n          }\n          resolvers[method as string].push(resolverObj)\n        })\n        pendingCalls[method as string].push(promise)\n      })\n    }) as any\n  }\n  \n  const asyncMock: AsyncMock<T> = {\n    implementation,\n    resolvers: resolvers as any,\n    waitForCall: async (method: string, callIndex = 0) => {\n      const calls = pendingCalls[method]\n      if (calls && calls[callIndex]) {\n        await calls[callIndex]\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  \n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { async: AsyncMock<T> }\n  layer.async = asyncMock\n  \n  return layer\n}\n\n/**\n * Example usage with a hypothetical UserService\n */\nexport Error>\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createConfigurableMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  config: MockConfig<T>\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> => {\\\\n  const implementation = {} as T\\\\n  \\\\n  for (const key in config.implementation) {\\\\n    const method = config.implementation[key]\\\\n    if (typeof method === 'function') {\\\\n      implementation[key] = ((...args: any[]) => {\\\\n        // Add configured delay\\\\n        const delay = config.delays?.[key] || 0\\\\n        \\\\n        // Check for configured error\\\\n        const error = config.errors?.[key]\\\\n        \\\\n        return pipe(\\\\n          Effect.sleep(delay),\\\\n          Effect.flatMap(() => \\\\n            error \\\\n              ? Effect.fail(error)\\\\n              : Effect.succeed(method(...args))\\\\n          )\\\\n        )\\\\n      }) as any\\\\n    } else {\\\\n      implementation[key] = method as any\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  return Layer.succeed(tag, implementation)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 3: Spy mock that records calls\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface SpyMock<T> {\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n  calls: Record<string, any[][]>\\\\n  resetCalls: () => void\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createSpyMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  baseImplementation: T\\\\n): method = baseImplementation[key]\\\\n    if (typeof method === 'function') {\\\\n      calls[key] = []\\\\n      implementation[key] = ((...args: any[]) => {\\\\n        calls[key].push(args)\\\\n        return method(...args)\\\\n      }) as any\\\\n    } else {\\\\n      implementation[key] = method\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const spy: SpyMock<T> = {\\\\n    implementation,\\\\n    calls,\\\\n    resetCalls: () => {\\\\n      for (const key in calls) {\\\\n        calls[key] = []\\\\n      }\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { spy: SpyMock<T> }\\\\n  layer.spy = spy\\\\n  \\\\n  return layer\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 4: State-based mock\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface StatefulMock<T, S> {\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n  getState: () => S\\\\n  setState: (state: S) => void\\\\n  resetState: () => void\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createStatefulMock = <T extends Record<string, any>, S>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  initialState: S,\\\\n  implementationFactory: (\\\\n    getState: () => S,\\\\n    setState: (state: S) => void\\\\n  ) => T\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { mock: StatefulMock<T, S> } => {\\\\n  let state = initialState\\\\n  \\\\n  const getState = () => state\\\\n  const setState = (newState: S) => { state = newState }\\\\n  const resetState = () => { state = initialState }\\\\n  \\\\n  const implementation = implementationFactory(getState, setState)\\\\n  \\\\n  const mock: StatefulMock<T, S> = {\\\\n    implementation,\\\\n    getState,\\\\n    setState,\\\\n    resetState\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { mock: StatefulMock<T, S> }\\\\n  layer.mock = mock\\\\n  \\\\n  return layer\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 5: Async mock with promise resolution control\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface AsyncMock<T> {\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n  resolvers: Record<string, {\\\\n    resolve: (value: any) => void\\\\n    reject: (error: any) => void\\\\n  }[]>\\\\n  waitForCall: (method: string, callIndex?: number) => Promise<void>\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createAsyncMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  methods: (keyof T)[]\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { async: AsyncMock<T> } => {\\\\n  const resolvers: Record<string, any[]> = {}\\\\n  const pendingCalls: Record<string, Promise<void>[]> = {}\\\\n  \\\\n  const implementation = {} as T\\\\n  \\\\n  for (const method of methods) {\\\\n    resolvers[method as string] = []\\\\n    pendingCalls[method as string] = []\\\\n    \\\\n    implementation[method as string] = (() => {\\\\n      return Effect.async((resume) => {\\\\n        const promise = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {\\\\n          const resolverObj = {\\\\n            resolve: (value: any) => {\\\\n              resume(Effect.succeed(value))\\\\n              resolve()\\\\n            },\\\\n            reject: (error: any) => {\\\\n              resume(Effect.fail(error))\\\\n              resolve()\\\\n            }\\\\n          }\\\\n          resolvers[method as string].push(resolverObj)\\\\n        })\\\\n        pendingCalls[method as string].push(promise)\\\\n      })\\\\n    }) as any\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const asyncMock: AsyncMock<T> = {\\\\n    implementation,\\\\n    resolvers: resolvers as any,\\\\n    waitForCall: async (method: string, callIndex = 0) => {\\\\n      const calls = pendingCalls[method]\\\\n      if (calls && calls[callIndex]) {\\\\n        await calls[callIndex]\\\\n      }\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { async: AsyncMock<T> }\\\\n  layer.async = asyncMock\\\\n  \\\\n  return layer\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Example usage with a hypothetical UserService\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface UserService {\\\\n  getUser: (id: string) => Effect.Effect<{ id: string; name: string }, Error>\\\\n  updateUser: (id: string, name: string) => Effect.Effect<void, Error>\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const UserService = Context.GenericTag<UserService>('UserService')\\\\n\\\\n// Example 1: Simple mock\\\\nexport const UserServiceSimpleMock = createSimpleMock(UserService, {\\\\n  getUser: (id: string) => Effect.succeed({ id, name: 'Test User' }),\\\\n  updateUser: () => Effect.succeed(undefined)\\\\n})\\\\n\\\\n// Example 2: Spy mock\\\\nexport const UserServiceSpyMock = createSpyMock(UserService, {\\\\n  getUser: (id: string) => Effect.succeed({ id, name: 'Test User' }),\\\\n  updateUser: () => Effect.succeed(undefined)\\\\n})\\\\n\\\\n// Example 3: Stateful mock\\\\nexport const UserServiceStatefulMock = createStatefulMock(\\\\n  UserService,\\\\n  { users: new Map<string, string>() },\\\\n  (getState, setState) => ({\\\\n    getUser: (id: string) =>\\\\n      Effect.gen(function* () {\\\\n        const state = getState()\\\\n        const name = string, name: string) =>\\\\n      Effect.sync(() => {\\\\n        const state = getState()\\\\n        state.users.set(id, name)\\\\n        setState(state)\\\\n      })\\\\n  })\\\\n)\\\\n\\\\n// Test helpers\\\\nexport const runEffectTest = async <A, E>(\\\\n  effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, any>,\\\\n  layer: Layer.Layer<any>\\\\n): Promise<A> => {\\\\n  return Effect.runPromise(pipe(effect, }\\\\n}\\\"}\"}}]","timestamp":1748307461}}

          "content": "# Effect-TS Cheat Sheet for OpenAgents Commander\n\nThis cheat sheet covers common Effect patterns and solutions to issues found in docs/fixes/.\n\n## Table of Contents\n1. [Service Creation Patterns](#service-creation-patterns)\n2. [Error Handling](#error-handling)  \n3. [Configuration Management](#configuration-management)\n4. [Testing Patterns](#testing-patterns)\n5. [React Integration](#react-integration)\n6. [Common Gotchas](#common-gotchas)\n\n## Service Creation Patterns\n\n### ✅ DO: Use Context.GenericTag\n```typescript\n// Correct - provides better type inference\nexport const MyService = Context.GenericTag<MyService>('MyService')\n```\n\n### ❌ DON'T: Use Context.Tag\n```typescript\n// Incorrect - can cause type inference issues\nexport const MyService = Context.Tag<MyService>('MyService')\n```\n\n### ✅ DO: Create specific error types\n```typescript\nexport class MyServiceError extends Error {\n  readonly _tag = 'MyServiceError'\n  constructor(message: string, readonly cause?: unknown) {\n    super(message)\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### ✅ DO: Use Layer.effect for complex services\n```typescript\nexport const MyServiceLive = Layer.effect(\n  MyService,\n  Effect.gen(function* () {\n    const config = yield* ConfigService\n    const database = yield* DatabaseService\n    return new MyServiceImpl(config, database)\n  })\n)\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n### Handle different error types\n```typescript\nEffect.catchTags({\n  ConfigError: (error) => Effect.fail(new MyServiceError('Invalid config', error)),\n  NetworkError: (error) => Effect.fail(new MyServiceError('Network failed', error)),\n  // Catch-all for unexpected errors\n  UnknownException: (error) => Effect.fail(new MyServiceError('Unknown error', error))\n})\n```\n\n### Convert Promise errors safely\n```typescript\n// ✅ DO: Wrap promises properly\nEffect.tryPromise({\n  try: () => fetch(url),\n  catch: (error) => new MyServiceError('Fetch failed', error)\n})\n\n// ❌ DON'T: Use Effect.promise directly\nEffect.promise(() => fetch(url)) // Loses error context\n```\n\n## Configuration Management\n\n### ✅ DO: Forbid fallbacks for sensitive data (Fix #022)\n```typescript\nexport const ApiKeyConfig = Config.string('API_KEY').pipe(\n  Config.validate({\n    message: 'API_KEY is required',\n    validation: (key) => key.length > 0\n  })\n)\n\n// ❌ DON'T: Use withDefault for secrets\nConfig.string('API_KEY').pipe(Config.withDefault('test-key'))\n```\n\n### ✅ DO: Create typed configuration services\n```typescript\nexport interface MyServiceConfig {\n  readonly apiUrl: string\n  readonly timeout: number\n}\n\nexport const MyServiceConfig = Context.GenericTag<MyServiceConfig>('MyServiceConfig')\n```\n\n## Testing Patterns\n\n### Use test layers properly\n```typescript\ndescribe('MyService', () => {\n  const testLayer = Layer.mergeAll(\n    DatabaseServiceTest,\n    ConfigServiceTest,\n    MyServiceLive\n  )\n  \n  it('should work', async () => {\n    const result = await Effect.runPromise(\n      myServiceMethod.pipe(Effect.provide(testLayer))\n    )\n    expect(result).toBe(expected)\n  })\n})\n```\n\n### Mock with spy capabilities\n```typescript\nconst mockLayer = createSpyMock(MyService, {\n  doSomething: () => Effect.succeed('mocked')\n})\n\n// After test\nexpect(mockLayer.spy.calls.doSomething).toHaveLength(1)\n```\n\n## React Integration\n\n### ✅ DO: Use refs to prevent stale closures (Fix #023)\n```typescript\nconst runtimeRef = useRef(runtime)\nuseEffect(() => {\n  runtimeRef.current = runtime\n}, [runtime])\n```\n\n### ✅ DO: Cleanup Effect fibers\n```typescript\nuseEffect(() => {\n  const fiber = Effect.runFork(myEffect)(runtime)\n  return () => {\n    fiber.interrupt\n  }\n}, [deps])\n```\n\n### ❌ DON'T: Create runtime in render\n```typescript\n// Bad - creates new runtime every render\nconst runtime = Runtime.make(layer)\n\n// Good - use useEffectRuntime hook\nconst runtime = useEffectRuntime(layer)\n```\n\n## Common Gotchas\n\n### 1. Double-yield in Layer.effect (Fix #014)\n```typescript\n// ❌ Wrong - double yield\nLayer.effect(\n  MyService,\n  Effect.gen(function* () {\n    return yield* Effect.succeed(new MyServiceImpl())\n  })\n)\n\n// ✅ Correct - single yield or direct return\nLayer.effect(\n  MyService,\n  Effect.gen(function* () {\n    const config = yield* ConfigService\n    return new MyServiceImpl(config)\n  })\n)\n```\n\n### 2. Service dependency cycles\n```typescript\n// ❌ Circular dependency\nconst ServiceA = Layer.effect(ServiceATag, \n  Effect.gen(function* () {\n    const b = yield* ServiceB // ServiceB depends on ServiceA!\n  })\n)\n\n// ✅ Use factory pattern or restructure\nconst ServiceAFactory = (serviceB: ServiceB) => \n  Layer.succeed(ServiceATag, new ServiceAImpl(serviceB))\n```\n\n### 3. Effect vs Promise in APIs\n```typescript\n// When exposing to non-Effect code\nexport class MyServiceBridge {\n  async doSomething(input: string): Promise<Result> {\n    return Effect.runPromise(\n      this.service.doSomething(input).pipe(\n        Effect.provide(this.runtime)\n      )\n    )\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Type inference with pipe\n```typescript\n// Help TypeScript with explicit types when needed\npipe(\n  myEffect,\n  Effect.map((value): ProcessedType => processValue(value)),\n  Effect.catchAll((error): Effect.Effect<Fallback, never> => \n    Effect.succeed(fallbackValue)\n  )\n)\n```\n\n### 5. Testing async Effects\n```typescript\n// Use Effect.runPromise in tests\nit('should handle async operations', async () => {\n  const result = await Effect.runPromise(\n    myAsyncEffect.pipe(Effect.provide(testLayer))\n  )\n  expect(result).toBe(expected)\n})\n```\n\n## Quick Reference\n\n### Essential imports\n```typescript\nimport { Context, Effect, Layer, pipe, Stream } from 'effect'\nimport * as Config from 'effect/Config'\nimport * as Runtime from 'effect/Runtime'\nimport * as Exit from 'effect/Exit'\n```\n\n### Service lifecycle\n1. Define interface → 2. Create Tag → 3. Implement → 4. Create Layer → 5. Compose\n\n### Effect operators by use case\n- **Transform**: `map`, `flatMap`, `tap`\n- **Error handling**: `catchAll`, `catchTags`, `orElse`\n- **Resource management**: `acquireRelease`, `scoped`\n- **Concurrency**: `all`, `race`, `forEach`\n- **Retry**: `retry`, `retryOrElse`\n- **Timeout**: `timeout`, `timeoutFail`\n\n### Debug helpers\n```typescript\n// Log intermediate values\nEffect.tap(value => Effect.log('Value:', value))\n\n// Log and pass through\nEffect.tapBoth({\n  onFailure: (error) => Effect.log('Error:', error),\n  onSuccess: (value) => Effect.log('Success:', value)\n})\n```"
        }

for using Effect services with automatic execution\\\\n */\\\\nexport function useEffectServiceAuto<A, E, R>(\\\\n  effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,\\\\n  runtime: Runtime.Runtime<R>,\\\\n  deps: React.DependencyList = []\\\\n) {\\\\n  const result = useEffectService(effect, runtime, deps)\\\\n  \\\\n  useEffect(() => {\\\\n    result.execute()\\\\n  }, [result.execute])\\\\n  \\\\n  return result\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Hook for creating a stable Effect runtime in React\\\\n * Prevents recreation on every render\\\\n */\\\\nexport function useEffectRuntime<R>(\\\\n  layer: Effect.Layer<R, never, never>,\\\\n  deps: React.DependencyList = []\\\\n): Runtime.Runtime<R> | null {\\\\n  const [runtime, setRuntime] = useState<Runtime.Runtime<R> | null>(null)\\\\n  \\\\n  useEffect(() => {\\\\n    let canceled = false\\\\n    \\\\n    Effect.runPromise(\\\\n      pipe(\\\\n        Effect.runtime<R>(),\\\\n        Effect.provide(layer)\\\\n      )\\\\n    ).then((rt) => {\\\\n      if (!canceled) {\\\\n        setRuntime(rt)\\\\n      }\\\\n    })\\\\n    \\\\n    return () => {\\\\n      canceled = true\\\\n    }\\\\n  }, deps)\\\\n  \\\\n  return runtime\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Hook for accessing a specific service from the runtime\\\\n */\\\\nexport function useService<T>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  runtime: Runtime.Runtime<any> | null\\\\n): T | null {\\\\n  const [service, setService] = useState<T | null>(null)\\\\n  \\\\n  useEffect(() => {\\\\n    if (runtime) {\\\\n      const context = runtime.context\\\\n      if (Context.isContext(context)) {\\\\n        const serviceInstance = Context.getOption(context, tag)\\\\n        if (serviceInstance._tag === 'Some') {\\\\n          setService(serviceInstance.value)\\\\n        }\\\\n      }\\\\n    }\\\\n  }, [runtime, tag])\\\\n  \\\\n  return service\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Hook for streaming Effect values\\\\n * Useful for [runtime])\\\\n  \\\\n  useEffect(() => {\\\\n    const fiber = Effect.runFork(\\\\n      stream.pipe(\\\\n        Effect.tap((value) => Effect.sync(() => onValue(value))),\\\\n        Effect.catchAll((error) =>\\\\n          Effect.sync(() => {\\\\n            if (onError) onError(error)\\\\n          })\\\\n        )\\\\n      )\\\\n    )(runtimeRef.current)\\\\n    \\\\n    return () => {\\\\n      fiber.interrupt\\\\n    }\\\\n  }, deps)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Error boundary component for Effect services\\\\n */\\\\nexport class EffectErrorBoundary extends React.Component<\\\\n  {\\\\n    children: React.ReactNode\\\\n    fallback: (error: unknown) => React.ReactNode\\\\n  },\\\\n  { hasError: boolean; error: unknown }\\\\n> {\\\\n  constructor(props: any) {\\\\n    super(props)\\\\n    this.state = { hasError: false, error: null }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  static getDerivedStateFromError(error: unknown) {\\\\n    return { hasError: true, error }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  componentDidCatch(error: Error, errorInfo: React.ErrorInfo) {\\\\n    console.error('Effect service error:', error, errorInfo)\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  render() {\\\\n    if * Test Service Mocks Template\\\\n * \\\\n * Patterns for creating test mocks that satisfy Effect's type system\\\\n * Addresses issues from docs/fixes/012-strategic-test-type-casting.md\\\\n */\\\\n\\\\nimport { Context, Effect, Layer, pipe } from 'effect'\\\\nimport type { ConfigError } from 'effect/ConfigError'\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 1: Simple mock with static values\\\\n */\\\\nexport const createSimpleMock = <T>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> => {\\\\n  return Layer.succeed(tag, implementation)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 2: Mock with configurable behavior\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface MockConfig<T> {\\\\n  implementation: Partial<T>\\\\n  delays?: Record<string, number>\\\\n  errors?: Record<string, Error>\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createConfigurableMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  config: MockConfig<T>\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> => {\\\\n  const implementation = {} as T\\\\n  \\\\n  for (const key in config.implementation) {\\\\n    const method = config.implementation[key]\\\\n    if (typeof method === 'function') {\\\\n      implementation[key] = ((...args: any[]) => {\\\\n        // Add configured delay\\\\n        const delay = config.delays?.[key] || 0\\\\n        \\\\n        // Check for configured error\\\\n        const error = config.errors?.[key]\\\\n        \\\\n        return pipe(\\\\n          Effect.sleep(delay),\\\\n          Effect.flatMap(() => \\\\n            error \\\\n              ? Effect.fail(error)\\\\n              : Effect.succeed(method(...args))\\\\n          )\\\\n        )\\\\n      }) as any\\\\n    } else {\\\\n      implementation[key] = method as any\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  return Layer.succeed(tag, implementation)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 3: Spy mock that records calls\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface SpyMock<T> {\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n  calls: Record<string, any[][]>\\\\n  resetCalls: () => void\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createSpyMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  baseImplementation: T\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { spy: SpyMock<T> } => {\\\\n  const calls: Record<string, any[][]> = {}\\\\n  \\\\n  const implementation = {} as T\\\\n  \\\\n  for (const key in baseImplementation) {\\\\n    const method = baseImplementation[key]\\\\n    if (typeof method === 'function') {\\\\n      calls[key] = []\\\\n      implementation[key] = ((...args: any[]) => {\\\\n        calls[key].push(args)\\\\n        return method(...args)\\\\n      }) as any\\\\n    } else {\\\\n      implementation[key] = method\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const spy: SpyMock<T> = {\\\\n    implementation,\\\\n    calls,\\\\n    resetCalls: () => {\\\\n      for (const key in calls) {\\\\n        calls[key] = []\\\\n      }\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { spy: SpyMock<T> }\\\\n  layer.spy = spy\\\\n  \\\\n  return layer\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 4: State-based mock\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface StatefulMock<T, S> {\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n  getState: () => S\\\\n  setState: (state: S) => void\\\\n  resetState: () => void\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createStatefulMock = <T extends Record<string, any>, S>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  initialState: S,\\\\n  implementationFactory: (\\\\n    getState: () => S,\\\\n    setState: (state: S) => void\\\\n  ) => T\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { mock: StatefulMock<T, S> } => {\\\\n  let state = initialState\\\\n  \\\\n  const getState = () => state\\\\n  const setState = (newState: S) => { state = newState }\\\\n  const resetState = () => { state = initialState }\\\\n  \\\\n  const implementation = implementationFactory(getState, setState)\\\\n  \\\\n  const mock: StatefulMock<T, S> = {\\\\n    implementation,\\\\n    getState,\\\\n    setState,\\\\n    resetState\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { mock: StatefulMock<T, S> }\\\\n  layer.mock = mock\\\\n  \\\\n  return layer\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Pattern 5: Async mock with promise resolution control\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface AsyncMock<T> {\\\\n  implementation: T\\\\n  resolvers: Record<string, {\\\\n    resolve: (value: any) => void\\\\n    reject: (error: any) => void\\\\n  }[]>\\\\n  waitForCall: (method: string, callIndex?: number) => Promise<void>\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const createAsyncMock = <T extends Record<string, any>>(\\\\n  tag: Context.Tag<T, T>,\\\\n  methods: (keyof T)[]\\\\n): Layer.Layer<T> & { async: AsyncMock<T> } => {\\\\n  const resolvers: Record<string, any[]> = {}\\\\n  const pendingCalls: Record<string, Promise<void>[]> = {}\\\\n  \\\\n  const implementation = {} as T\\\\n  \\\\n  for (const method of methods) {\\\\n    resolvers[method as string] = []\\\\n    pendingCalls[method as string] = []\\\\n    \\\\n    implementation[method as string] = (() => {\\\\n      return Effect.async((resume) => {\\\\n        const promise = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {\\\\n          const resolverObj = {\\\\n            resolve: (value: any) => {\\\\n              resume(Effect.succeed(value))\\\\n              resolve()\\\\n            },\\\\n            reject: (error: any) => {\\\\n              resume(Effect.fail(error))\\\\n              resolve()\\\\n            }\\\\n          }\\\\n          resolvers[method as string].push(resolverObj)\\\\n        })\\\\n        pendingCalls[method as string].push(promise)\\\\n      })\\\\n    }) as any\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const asyncMock: AsyncMock<T> = {\\\\n    implementation,\\\\n    resolvers: resolvers as any,\\\\n    waitForCall: async (method: string, callIndex = 0) => {\\\\n      const calls = pendingCalls[method]\\\\n      if (calls && calls[callIndex]) {\\\\n        await calls[callIndex]\\\\n      }\\\\n    }\\\\n  }\\\\n  \\\\n  const layer = Layer.succeed(tag, implementation) as Layer.Layer<T> & { async: AsyncMock<T> }\\\\n  layer.async = asyncMock\\\\n  \\\\n  return layer\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n/**\\\\n * Example usage with a hypothetical UserService\\\\n */\\\\nexport interface UserService {\\\\n  getUser: (id: string) => Effect.Effect<{ id: string; name: string }, Error>\\\\n  updateUser: (id: string, name: string) => Effect.Effect<void, Error>\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const UserService = Context.GenericTag<UserService>('UserService')\\\\n\\\\n// Example 1: Simple mock\\\\nexport const UserServiceSimpleMock = createSimpleMock(UserService, {\\\\n  getUser: (id: string) => Effect.succeed({ id, name: 'Test User' }),\\\\n  updateUser: () => Effect.succeed(undefined)\\\\n})\\\\n\\\\n// Example 2: Spy mock\\\\nexport const UserServiceSpyMock = createSpyMock(UserService, {\\\\n  getUser: (id: string) => Effect.succeed({ id, name: 'Test User' }),\\\\n  updateUser: () => Effect.succeed(undefined)\\\\n})\\\\n\\\\n// Example 3: Stateful mock\\\\nexport const UserServiceStatefulMock = createStatefulMock(\\\\n  UserService,\\\\n  { users: new Map<string, string>() },\\\\n  (getState, setState) => ({\\\\n    getUser: (id: string) =>\\\\n      Effect.gen(function* () {\\\\n        const state = getState()\\\\n        const name = state.users.get(id)\\\\n        if (!name) {\\\\n          return yield* Effect.fail(new Error('User not found'))\\\\n        }\\\\n        return { id, name }\\\\n      }),\\\\n    updateUser: (id: string, name: string) =>\\\\n      Effect.sync(() => {\\\\n        const state = getState()\\\\n        state.users.set(id, name)\\\\n        setState(state)\\\\n      })\\\\n  })\\\\n)\\\\n\\\\n// Test helpers\\\\nexport const runEffectTest = async <A, E>(\\\\n  effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, any>,\\\\n  layer: Layer.Layer<any>\\\\n): Promise<A> => {\\\\n  return Effect.runPromise(pipe(effect, Effect.provide(layer)))\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\nexport const runEffectTestExpectError = async <A, E>(\\\\n  effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, any>,\\\\n  layer: Layer.Layer<any>\\\\n): Promise<E> => {\\\\n  try {\\\\n    await Effect.runPromise(pipe(effect, Effect.provide(layer)))\\\\n    throw new Error('Expected effect to fail')\\\\n  } catch (error) {\\\\n    return error as E\\\\n  }\\\\n}\\\"}\"}}]","timestamp":1748307511}}
